# Corrected prefetch transfer: functional pass, timings discarded

The prospective speed pair is unusable as a comparison. Its uncorrected arm completed inside the physical ceiling, but system-wide swap-outs rose by 19,760 pages (16,384 bytes per page). The corrected arm was then refused before launch when the desktop did not sustain the existing quiet admission period. No partial timing subset, post-hoc tolerance or replacement speed cells are used. This source's discarded flag applies to every timing below, including the separate functional-only runs.

The first functional attempt safely refused before allocation: the native planner admitted only the historical uncorrected reserve. The repaired path uses a separate corrected memory-profile identity whose exact reservation derives from the authenticated correction header and persists through governor replanning. Tests reject off/automatic/changed reservations and preserve the historical native profile. An incremental-build catalogue crash was also preserved; rebuilding all project modules without changing source bytes restores the complete catalogue. No model was loaded by those crashed checks.

The separate corrected functional run preserves exact committed token IDs and text on all three existing workloads and the complete coding answer. It proves loading, charged memory and this bounded output-invariance scope. It proves no speed benefit and does not qualify the candidate for production or Auto. The original remains the supported/default pack. The full twenty-token target remains unmet; only a fresh prospective clean paired run could establish whether this forecast transfer helps. No further timing attempt occurs in this session.
